# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO based on the 'canApplyForIPO' flag and possibly other business rules (e.g., not restricted due to failed attempts).
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.canApplyForIPO (boolean).
     (Inferred: The bank sets this flag to false if the customer has too many failed attempts; otherwise true.)
Related Features: this.canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean – true if customer is eligible, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of paid amounts for all approved IPO applications of the customer.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. Iterate through this.applications.
  3. For each application with status == APPROVAL, add application.amountOfMoney to total.
  4. Return total.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::status, Application::amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double – total amount of approved applications (>=0).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for a retail customer after verifying eligibility and no duplicate approved applications for the same company.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate input: shares > 0, amount > 0, doc != null.
  2. Call this.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
  3. Check if any application in this.applications has status == APPROVAL and company.name == company.name; if yes, return false (duplicate approved application).
  4. Create a new Application object:
     - Set amountOfMoney = amount
     - Set share = shares
     - Set status = PENDING
     - Set allowance = doc
     - Set company = company
     - Set customer = this
     - Initialize emails as empty list
  5. Add the new application to this.applications.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: this.isEligibleForIPO(), this.applications, Application constructor
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input: company : Company, shares : int (>0), amount : double (>0), doc : Document (non-null)
Output: boolean – true if application created successfully, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Customer must be eligible (isEligibleForIPO returns true). No existing approved application for the same company. Inputs valid.
Post-condition: If successful, a new PENDING application is added to this.applications list.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Allows a customer to cancel a pending application for a given company by delegating to the Application's cancel method.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate through this.applications list.
  2. Find an application where application.company.name == companyName AND application.status == PENDING.
  3. If found, call application.cancel() and return its result (true/false).
  4. If not found, return false.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::cancel
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String – the name of the company for which to cancel the application.
Output: boolean – true if cancellation succeeds, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Customer must have at least one application with the given company name that is in PENDING status.
Post-condition: If successful, the matching application's status is set to CANCELED.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the total number of IPO applications that have been completed (approved or rejected), excluding pending and canceled ones.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. Iterate through this.applications.
  3. For each application, if status == APPROVAL or status == REJECTED, increment count.
  4. Return count.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::status
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int – count of approved or rejected applications (>=0).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer

    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}

    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications

Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO based on the 'canApplyForIPO' flag and possibly other business rules (e.g., not restricted due to failed attempts).
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.canApplyForIPO (boolean).
     (Inferred: The bank sets this flag to false if the customer has too many failed attempts; otherwise true.)
Related Features: this.canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean – true if customer is eligible, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of paid amounts for all approved IPO applications of the customer.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. Iterate through this.applications.
  3. For each application with status == APPROVAL, add application.amountOfMoney to total.
  4. Return total.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::status, Application::amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double – total amount of approved applications (>=0).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for a retail customer after verifying eligibility and no duplicate approved applications for the same company.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate input: shares > 0, amount > 0, doc != null.
  2. Call this.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
  3. Check if any application in this.applications has status == APPROVAL and company.name == company.name; if yes, return false (duplicate approved application).
  4. Create a new Application object:
     - Set amountOfMoney = amount
     - Set share = shares
     - Set status = PENDING
     - Set allowance = doc
     - Set company = company
     - Set customer = this
     - Initialize emails as empty list
  5. Add the new application to this.applications.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: this.isEligibleForIPO(), this.applications, Application constructor
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input: company : Company, shares : int (>0), amount : double (>0), doc : Document (non-null)
Output: boolean – true if application created successfully, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Customer must be eligible (isEligibleForIPO returns true). No existing approved application for the same company. Inputs valid.
Post-condition: If successful, a new PENDING application is added to this.applications list.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Allows a customer to cancel a pending application for a given company by delegating to the Application's cancel method.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate through this.applications list.
  2. Find an application where application.company.name == companyName AND application.status == PENDING.
  3. If found, call application.cancel() and return its result (true/false).
  4. If not found, return false.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::cancel
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String – the name of the company for which to cancel the application.
Output: boolean – true if cancellation succeeds, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Customer must have at least one application with the given company name that is in PENDING status.
Post-condition: If successful, the matching application's status is set to CANCELED.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the total number of IPO applications that have been completed (approved or rejected), excluding pending and canceled ones.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. Iterate through this.applications.
  3. For each application, if status == APPROVAL or status == REJECTED, increment count.
  4. Return count.
Related Features: this.applications, Application::status
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int – count of approved or rejected applications (>=0).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: No changes to system state.

# Operation Specifications
Application::approve
Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status to APPROVAL, and sends notification emails to both the customer and the company.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if application status is PENDING; if not, return false.
  2. Retrieve the associated customer via this.customer.
  3. Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
  4. Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
  5. Create and send two emails:
     - One to customer (content generated by Email::createEmailContent with approval info)
     - One to company (content generated similarly)
     Add each email to this.emails.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: this.status, this.customer, this.company, this.share, this.amountOfMoney, this.emails
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the Application instance)
Output: boolean – true if approval succeeds, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Application status must be PENDING. Customer must be eligible for IPO (isEligibleForIPO() returns true).
Post-condition: If successful, status is APPROVAL, two emails are added to this.emails. If failed, no changes.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application by setting its status to a canceled state (implicitly removing from active processing).
Algorithm:
  1. Check if this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING; if not, return false.
  2. Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.CANCELED (assumed enum value, though not shown in diagram; infer as PENDING -> canceled).
  3. Return true.
Related Features: this.status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean – true if cancellation succeeds, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Application status must be PENDING.
Post-condition: If successful, status is set to CANCELED (inferred). No emails are sent.

Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application, updates its status to REJECTED, and sends a rejection email to the customer with full details.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING; if not, return false.
  2. Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.
  3. Call this.sendRejectionEmail() to compose and send the rejection email.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: this.status, this.customer, this.company, this.share, this.amountOfMoney, this.emails
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean – true if rejection succeeds, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Application status must be PENDING.
Post-condition: Status is REJECTED, and a rejection email (with customer name, surname, email, telephone, company name, shares, paid amount) is added to this.emails.

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Creates and sends a rejection email to the customer containing the customer's personal details, company name, number of shares, and paid amount.
Algorithm:
  1. Retrieve customer info: name, surname, email, telephone from this.customer.
  2. Retrieve company name from this.company.name.
  3. Retrieve this.share and this.amountOfMoney.
  4. Compose email content string using Email::createEmailContent (or manually) including: customer's name, surname, email, telephone, company name, shares, amount.
  5. Create a new Email object with receiver = this.customer.email and content = composed string.
  6. Add this email to this.emails list.
  7. Return (void).
Related Features: this.customer, this.company, this.share, this.amountOfMoney, this.emails
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Application must have a valid customer, company, share, and amount.
Post-condition: A rejection email is added to this.emails with content containing all required fields.

Post-condition: A rejection email is added to this.emails with content containing all required fields.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  {
    // 1. Validate input: shares > 0, amount > 0, doc != null.
    if (shares <= 0 || amount <= 0 || doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    // 2. Call this.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
    if (!isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;

    if (applications != null)
    {
      for (Application app : applications)
      {
        if (app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL && app.getCompany().getName().equals(company.getName()))
        {
          return false;
        }
      }
    }
    // 4. Create a new Application object
    Application newApplication = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    newApplication.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    newApplication.setShare(shares);
    newApplication.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    newApplication.setAllowance(doc);
    newApplication.setCompany(company);
    newApplication.setCustomer(this);
    // Initialize emails as empty list (already initialized by factory)
    // 5. Add the new application to this.applications.
    getApplications().add(newApplication);
    // 6. Return true.
    return true;

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  1.4 Generate the class summarization.

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status to APPROVAL, and sends notification emails to both the customer and the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Check if application status is PENDING; if not, return false.
   *   2. Retrieve the associated customer via this.customer.
```

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
public boolean approve()
{
    // Check if application status is PENDING
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
        return false;
    }
    // Retrieve the associated customer
```

```java
public boolean approve()
{
    // Check if application status is PENDING; if not, return false.
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
        return false;
    }
    // Retrieve the associated customer via this.customer.
    Customer customer = getCustomer();
    // Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
    if (!customer.isEligibleForIPO()) {
        return false;
    }
    // Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);
    // Create and send two emails:
    // One to customer (content generated by Email::createEmailContent with approval info)
    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setReceiver(customer.getEmail());
    customerEmail.setContent(customerEmail.createEmailContent(getCompany(), getShare(), customer, getAmountOfMoney()));
    getEmails().add(customerEmail);
    // One to company (content generated similarly)
    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setReceiver(getCompany().getEmail());
    companyEmail.setContent(companyEmail.createEmailContent(getCompany(), getShare(), customer, getAmountOfMoney()));
    getEmails().add(companyEmail);
    // Return true.
    return true;
}

public boolean reject()
{
    // Check if this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING; if not, return false.
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
        return false;
    }
    // Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.REJECTED);
    // Call this.sendRejectionEmail() to compose and send the rejection email.
    sendRejectionEmail();
    // Return true.
    return true;
}

public boolean cancel()
{
    // Check if this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING; if not, return false.
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
        return false;
    }
    // Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.CANCELED.
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.CANCELED);
    // Return true.
    return true;
}

public void sendRejectionEmail()
{
    // Retrieve customer info: name, surname, email, telephone from this.customer.
    Customer customer = getCustomer();
    String name = customer.getName();
    String surname = customer.getSurname();
    String email = customer.getEmail();
    String telephone = customer.getTelephone();
    // Retrieve company name from this.company.name.
    String companyName = getCompany().getName();
    // Retrieve this.share and this.amountOfMoney.
    int shares = getShare();
    double amount = getAmountOfMoney();
    // Compose email content string including: customer's name, surname, email, telephone, company name, shares, amount.
    String content = "Dear " + name + " " + surname + ",\n" +
                     "Your IPO application for " + companyName + " has been rejected.\n" +
                     "Details:\n" +
                     "Name: " + name + "\n" +
                     "Surname: " + surname + "\n" +
                     "Email: " + email + "\n" +
                     "Telephone: " + telephone + "\n" +
                     "Company: " + companyName + "\n" +
                     "Shares: " + shares + "\n" +
                     "Paid Amount: " + amount;
    // Create a new Email object with receiver = this.customer.email and content = composed string.
    Email rejectionEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    rejectionEmail.setReceiver(email);
    rejectionEmail.setContent(content);
    // Add this email to this.emails list.
    getEmails().add(rejectionEmail);
    // Return (void).
}
```

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status to APPROVAL, and sends notification emails to both the customer and the company.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Check if application status is PENDING; if not, return false.
   *   2. Retrieve the associated customer via this.customer.
   *   3. Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
   *   4. Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
   *   5. Create and send two emails:
   *      - One to customer (content generated by Email::createEmailContent with approval info)
   *      - One to company (content generated similarly)
   *      Add each email to this.emails.
   *   6. Return true.
   * Related Features: this.status, this.customer, this.company, this.share, this.amountOfMoney, this.emails
   * Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None (operates on the Application instance)
   * Output: boolean – true if approval succeeds, false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: Application status must be PENDING. Customer must be eligible for IPO (isEligibleForIPO() returns true).
   * Post-condition: If successful, status is APPROVAL, two emails are added to this.emails. If failed, no changes.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    // Check if application status is PENDING; if not, return false.
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    // Retrieve the associated customer via this.customer.
    Customer customer = getCustomer();
    // Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(); if false, return false.
    if (!customer.isEligibleForIPO()) {
      return false;
    }
    // Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);
    // Create and send two emails:
    //   - One to customer (content generated by Email::createEmailContent with approval info)
    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setReceiver(customer.getEmail());
    customerEmail.setContent(customerEmail.createEmailContent(getCompany(), getShare(), customer, getAmountOfMoney()));
    getEmails().add(customerEmail);
    //   - One to company (content generated similarly)
    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setReceiver(getCompany().getEmail());
    companyEmail.setContent(companyEmail.createEmailContent(getCompany(), getShare(), customer, getAmountOfMoney()));
    getEmails().add(companyEmail);
    // Return true.
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

      return false;

    // Set this.status = ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Creates and sends a rejection email to the customer containing the customer's personal details, company name, number of shares, and paid amount.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Retrieve customer info: name, surname, email, telephone from this.customer.
   *   2. Retrieve company name from this.company.name.
   *   3. Retrieve this.share and this.amountOfMoney.
   *   4. Compose email content string using Email::createEmailContent (or manually) including: customer's name, surname, email, telephone, company name, shares, amount.
   *   5. Create a new Email object with receiver = this.customer.email and content = composed string.
   *   6. Add this email to this.emails list.
   *   7. Return (void).
   * Related Features: this.customer, this.company, this.share, this.amountOfMoney, this.emails
   * Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
   * Input: None
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: Application must have a valid customer, company, share, and amount.
   * Post-condition: A rejection email is added to this.emails with content containing all required fields.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void sendRejectionEmail()
  {
    // Retrieve customer info: name, surname, email, telephone from this.customer.
    Customer customer = getCustomer();
    String name = customer.getName();
    String surname = customer.getSurname();
    String email = customer.getEmail();
    String telephone = customer.getTelephone();
    // Retrieve company name from this.company.name.
    String companyName = getCompany().getName();
    // Retrieve this.share and this.amountOfMoney.
    int shares = getShare();
    double amount = getAmountOfMoney();
    // Compose email content string including: customer's name, surname, email, telephone, company name, shares, amount.
    String content = "Dear " + name + " " + surname + ",\n" +
                     "Your IPO application for " + companyName + " has been rejected.\n" +
                     "Details:\n" +
                     "Name: " + name + "\n" +
                     "Surname: " + surname + "\n" +
                     "Email: " + email + "\n" +
                     "Telephone: " + telephone + "\n" +
                     "Company: " + companyName + "\n" +
                     "Shares: " + shares + "\n" +
                     "Paid Amount: " + amount;
    // Create a new Email object with receiver = this.customer.email and content = composed string.
    Email rejectionEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    rejectionEmail.setReceiver(email);
    rejectionEmail.setContent(content);
    // Add this email to this.emails list.
    getEmails().add(rejectionEmail);
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
